Running the real `pnpm build` surfaced two P0 build failures the type-checker missed: (1) **storefront middleware/proxy conflict** — Next.js 16 renamed the middleware entry to `proxy.ts` and **errors the build when both `middleware.ts` and `proxy.ts` exist**; the storefront still carried the Next-15-era `middleware.ts` re-export shim. Removed it (proxy.ts is self-sufficient: `export async function proxy` + `export const config` matcher — verified live by the build reporting `ƒ Proxy (Middleware)`); updated `lb1-storefront-middleware` test to the Next-16 reality. (2) **jsdom in the client bundle** — `theme-engine`'s barrel re-exports the Liquid renderer, whose server-only `await import('jsdom')` (SSR DOMPurify) got pulled into all 19 client section components via `DEFAULT_THEME_SETTINGS`, breaking the client build on jsdom/undici `node:*` schemes. Fixed at the source with `import(/* webpackIgnore: true */ 'jsdom')` (behavior-preserving — server still requires jsdom at runtime; client never bundles it) + `serverExternalPackages:['jsdom']`. **Verified:** storefront/merchant/identity/marketing/platform-console all `next build` **Compiled successfully**; theme-engine 56/56; api-gateway tsc 0; launch 143/143; **gate 107/107**. | V1-COMPLETION-REFUND-RESTORE | **Gift-card refund restore — closes the consumer-money gap.** Certification found `refund_credit` was a dead ledger capability: `create_checkout_order` debits the gift-card ledger on redemption, but no path wrote the offsetting `refund_credit` the balance projection already counts — so refunding/cancelling a gift-card-funded order **silently destroyed the customer's redeemed balance** (`complete_order_refund` writes nothing to the gift-card ledger). Migration 174 adds `restore_gift_card_on_refund(store, order)` (SECURITY DEFINER): for each **committed** redemption on the order (FOR UPDATE) it writes a `refund_credit` entry (`amount` = redeemed, `balance_after` = projection + amount) and marks the redemption `released`. **Idempotent** (unique `gift_card.refund_credit:order:redemption` key + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` + committed→released transition → no double credit). Wired into the `PATCH /orders/:id` cancel/refund transition (the unambiguous whole-order-reversed signal) via `db.rpc`, best-effort. Canonical `complete_order_refund`/`create_checkout_order` **untouched** (validator-pinned). Partial refunds of gift-card orders deliberately not auto-split (documented boundary, avoids guessing tender allocation). **Verified:** api-gateway tsc 0; canonical-ledger-only PASS; sequential/migration-safety/rollback PASS; `lb11-refund-restore` (6); launch 143/143; **gate 107/107**. | `database/supabase/migrations/174_v1_gift_card_refund_restore.sql`, `services/api-gateway/src/routes/orders.ts` | `database/supabase/migrations/174_v1_gift_card_refund_restore.sql`; `database/supabase/rollback/174_v1_gift_card_refund_restore.rollback.sql` | `tests/launch/lb11-refund-restore.test.mjs` (6) | canonical-ledger-only, sequential-migration, migration-safety, rollback-integrity; api-gateway tsc 0; launch 143/143; **gate 107/107 PASS** | DONE | this commit |
| V1-LAUNCH-LB11 | **LB11_GIFTCARDS_COMPLETE.** Gift cards were structurally broken: issuance wrote the legacy `public.gift_cards` table (plaintext code, `store_id`), but the canonical checkout redemption (`create_checkout_order`) reads `gift_card_accounts` (hashed `code_hash`, `site_id`) + projects balance from `gift_card_ledger_entries` — **two disconnected models, so an issued card could never be redeemed.** Completed the feature by cutting issuance + lifecycle over to the SAME canonical model the checkout already expects. **Migration 173** (additive, replayable, `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` only — all 3 tables + RLS already exist) adds 6 SECURITY DEFINER RPCs: `issue_gift_card` (atomic account + `issued` ledger entry; hashes the code with the **identical** `encode(digest(code,'sha256'),'hex')` the redemption looks up by → the issued card is immediately redeemable; idempotent; returns the plaintext **once**), `gift_card_available_balance` (the same ledger projection as redemption — balance is never a mutable decrement), `list_gift_cards` (projected balances + redemption counts), `check_gift_card_balance` (masked validate by code), `set_gift_card_status` (fraud hold/disable/reactivate — status gates redemption, writes an audit `fraud_hold`/`release` entry, never burns balance), `gift_card_transactions` (ledger + order-linked redemption audit). **Gateway** `routes/gift-cards.ts` (extracted to keep products.ts ≤1500; now 1229) drives all of it **via `db.rpc` only** (no direct `.from('gift_card_accounts').insert/update` → canonical-ledger-only validator clean); `products.ts` delegates via `registerGiftCardRoutes(router)` and **no longer writes `gift_cards`**. Recipient email dispatch (`enqueueGiftCardIssuedEmail` → `email.gift_card_issued`, worker recognises it). commerce-core schemas (recipient + status + balance) + api-client (`balance`/`transactions`/`setStatus`). MCP UI: reveal the code **once**, recipient field, projected balance, transactions drawer (ledger + redemptions), disable/reactivate. New app-api proxy `gift-cards/[id]/route.ts` (GET audit + PATCH status) → **route lockstep 128→129** (domain-boundary, federation, lb6, lb12 mirror, ownership doc). Tenant isolation: every route `verifySiteOwnership`; RPCs site-scoped SECURITY DEFINER. **Verified:** api-gateway tsc 0, queue-workers tsc 0, api-client `tsc -b` 0, MCP gift-cards page + new route tsc 0 (3 pre-existing unrelated MCP errors untouched); `lb11-giftcards` (11: canonical RPCs, hash-match lifecycle close, ledger projection, RPC-only mutations, ownership, fraud, email, schemas/client, UI reveal, rollback+lockstep); money B3-B5 convergence 4/4 (redemption guard intact); launch suite 137/137; **gate 107/107 PASS**. create_checkout_order untouched. Live ESP send gated by `HR-ESP-LIVE`. | S2-ROUTE-TEMPLATE-CLOSURE | **Closed the D3a/D3b defects; found D3c already fixed.** D3a: `resolveLiquidTemplateKey` silently substituted `bundle.templates.index` (or an arbitrary key) for any unmatched route template -- a Liquid-enabled store showed the merchant's home page (or a random other template) under any URL whose template was missing, a wrong-page bug with SEO impact. Fixed by making the function return `string \| null` (no substitution) and adding `StorefrontRenderPlan.liquidTemplateMiss: boolean` -- true only when the store runs Liquid, the route needed a specific template, the bundle has no match, AND no `fallbackSections` were supplied (a real, honest miss); false whenever real fallback content exists (the existing plain/legacy chain already prefers `fallbackSections` over its homepage-sections catch-all, so CMS/collection/shop routes now get REAL content instead of the home page on a miss, strictly better than before). The render-FAILURE-\>React safety net in composition-engine.tsx (`renderLiquidPlan`'s catch block, the F5 chrome-fallback seam) is a different, correct seam and was left untouched, verified by regression test. D3b: re-derived the missing-route list myself instead of trusting the prompt -- confirmed 5 of the 6 named templates were genuinely routeless (`search`, `list-collections`, `contact`, `password`, `customers/register`); the 6th, **`policy`, was already reachable** via `/privacy`, `/returns`, `/shipping`, `/terms`, which already pass `routeType="policy"` through `ContentResourceRenderer` -\> `resolveStorefrontTemplate` -- did NOT add a `/policies/[slug]` route, which would have created a second system for one concern (one-system-per-domain-validator re-run clean, confirming this call was right). A CTO review verified this and additionally corrected a misattribution in that report: `checkout.ts`'s hosted-checkout caller was flagged as a second blocker but is not one -- independently re-verified in this phase (`normalizeHostedCheckoutPaymentProvider` at `checkout.ts:144-150` already returns only `razorpay`/`cod`, `null` otherwise, and `buildHostedCheckoutOrderCommand` short-circuits with `CHECKOUT_PAYMENT_METHOD_UNSUPPORTED` before ever reaching the RPC). **W1** -- added `normalizeDraftOrderPaymentMethod` (`orders.ts`, mirrors the `checkout.ts` normalizer pattern) and wired it into the convert handler: rejects with `DRAFT_PAYMENT_METHOD_RETIRED` (409) before the RPC is ever called. Deliberately does not silently coerce a retired value to `'cod'` -- that would change how the customer pays without anyone being told, the "stub reads as success" failure the honesty law forbids. **W2** -- migration `201_narrow_draft_orders_payment_method.sql` narrows `draft_orders_payment_method_check` to `razorpay`/`cod` via `ADD CONSTRAINT ... NOT VALID` (deliberate: a plain `ADD CONSTRAINT` fails outright against any pre-M1 row that violates it; `NOT VALID` enforces new/updated rows only, without backfilling, discarding, or mutating any existing row -- that stays a separate, explicitly out-of-scope product/data decision). Noted the old constraint also inconsistently omitted `'paypal'` while allowing `'stripe'`/`'cashfree'` -- evidence it was never actively maintained. **W3** -- migration `202_narrow_checkout_order_payment_method.sql` narrows the RPC's own guard to `razorpay`/`cod`. The function body is migration 170's reproduced byte-for-byte except the guard line, verified via a full-body diff during authoring (the only other difference is the `DROP FUNCTION` signature, necessarily adapted since 202 targets the current 16-param signature, not 170's stale 14-param one). No CHECK constraint was added to `orders.payment_method` -- existing order rows legitimately carry retired rails from years under the wider guard; that remains a separate, deliberately-scoped follow-up with its own backfill plan. **W4** -- both migrations have faithful paired rollbacks, each diffed against the original constraint/function body they restore. **W5** -- application-layer: direct unit tests of `normalizeDraftOrderPaymentMethod` (razorpay/cod pass through, every retired rail incl. stripe/paypal/cashfree/adyen/square/braintree normalizes to `null`) plus a structural/ordering source assertion proving the `DRAFT_PAYMENT_METHOD_RETIRED` guard precedes `service.createOrder(` in the handler -- both observed passing. A2 trust-badges/A5 app-block-slot/A6 skills-section were tag-shape or missing-fallback gaps (`<strong>`->`<h3>`; a bare empty `<div>` app-block placeholder -> the same "Installed app" fallback title app-platform.ts already uses elsewhere, fixed centrally in `resolveLiquidAppBlockHtml` so every `@app` slot benefits, not just this one section; unconditional "General" grouping heading, matching skills-section.tsx's actual default for every currently-possible skill block, since the Liquid schema declares no category field to group by). A3/A4 featured-products/product-grid empty-states required discovering that `product-card` is a REGISTERED Liquid block (`blocks/product-card.liquid`) that always emits a structural `<article>` from `block.settings` even with nothing configured -- checking `block.content` for blankness (the first attempt) could never fire; the working fix checks each block's own `title` setting instead, and precisely mirrors each React component's real behavior (featured-products fully suppresses header on empty, matching its early return; product-grid keeps its header and adds an empty message alongside, matching its non-early-return). **Group B (4, neither engine)**: B1 hero height / B2 hero textAlign were declared-but-ignored in BOTH engines' single-banner path (only the separate BannerCarousel sub-component read them) -- implemented as real CSS-class-driven behavior in both `hero.tsx` and hero-banner.liquid (+ matching `LIQUID_THEME_BASE_CSS`/`liquid-theme.css` rules); B3 about-section layout was already functionally implemented in React (branches into 3 different JSX structures) but never in Liquid -- fixed there only. All three remain "absent from both" in the harness's own text-scraping matrix even after the fix, because a CSS class is not a text/link/image node the matrix's regex-based extractor can see -- this is a proven, permanent limitation of that specific detection method, not evidence the fix is incomplete; closed the actual gap the S3 brief asked for -- a new `verifyBehavioralSettingChanges` step in the harness itself renders each setting's two values and diffs the raw HTML output directly (6/6 PASS, including B4). B4 app-block-slot's `slot` field is internal placement routing metadata (e.g. `storefront.home.after-hero`), confirmed never rendered by either engine and confirmed it SHOULD NOT be -- excluded from the harness's comparison via a named, commented `PARITY_EXCLUDED_SETTINGS` map (one field, one section, does not touch the general comparator). **Group C (1)**: banner-carousel's "crash" was diagnosed as a harness fixture bug, not a component defect -- `renderer-registry.tsx` has no entry for it at all (confirmed by inspection), `hero.tsx` is its sole real caller and only invokes it, already prop-translated, when `banners.length > 1`; the harness previously fed it the generic `{section, theme, products, basePath}` shape every other section gets, leaving its actual required `banners` prop undefined. Dropped the explicit extensions; did not touch `allowImportingTsExtensions`. Confirmed the test still runs and passes (6/6) -- but only via `npx tsx --tsconfig apps/storefront-runtime/tsconfig.json --test <file>`; plain `node --test` cannot execute this file at all (no TS/JSX transform, no `@/*` alias resolution) regardless of the extension fix -- the phase prompt's literal verification command was imprecise on this point, noted rather than silently worked around. **Item 3** -- the one remaining React-only parity diff (`banner-carousel`: React renders the active slide's heading as an `<h1>`, Liquid rendered it as a bare `<span>`). Chose decision (a), implement it, over excluding the section: Liquid's `<span>` became `<h3>`, matching the SAME per-item-title-as-h3 convention this theme already uses everywhere else a list-of-items sits under a section-level `<h2>` (trust-badges/skills-section/portfolio-grid/experience-timeline) -- not a new pattern, and more honest than declaring the comparison invalid, since the actual heading-text gap is now genuinely closed (an `<h1>`-per-slide copy of React's single-active-slide UX was rejected as a real a11y anti-pattern for Liquid's static all-slides-at-once list, not merely cosmetic). banner-carousel's structural oddity -- React's side is not a registered storefront section at all (absent from `renderer-registry.tsx`; only reachable via hero.tsx's multi-banner branch) -- is unchanged and separately documented, but does not make its own heading semantics wrong to fix. **Item 4** -- re-ran the harness: text-scraping matrix holds at 8/20 (unchanged count, but featured-products and banner-carousel both moved from a "missing/wrong content" reason to an accepted Liquid-richer-only reason set, confirmed via full per-section reason diffing, not just the headline number); added 5 new harness-level behavioral checks (`verifyFeaturedProductsHeaderSurvivesEmptyState`, 4 sub-checks) proving the Item-1 fix on BOTH engines directly (not inferable from the text matrix, same blind-spot reasoning as S3's B1-B3), bringing the behavioral suite to 10/10. `docs/evidence/liquid-parity/S3-render-parity.json` regenerated in place (this is the harness's own designated S3 evidence file, already distinct from S1's preserved 5/20 baseline -- no separate "S3-FIX" evidence file was created, since this phase fixes S3's OWN in-flight work rather than superseding a prior phase's frozen baseline). Both example bundles re-verified byte-identical in `files/sections`/`files/blocks`, before and after, by the shell one-liner and the existing regression test. `letbuyy-core` confirmed still DARK; S1's adversarial suite (14/14, includes the letbuyy-core-attach dark-canary test) re-run clean. | `packages/theme-engine/src/theme-bundle.ts`, `apps/storefront-runtime/src/components/sections/featured-products.tsx`, `apps/storefront-runtime/src/app/demo/urban-kicks/page.test.ts`, `apps/storefront-runtime/scripts/liquid-parity-harness.mjs`, 14 `packages/theme-engine/examples/{letbuyy-core-liquid,urban-kicks-liquid}/**` files (regenerated via `generate-examples.mjs`) | None | `packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-s3-render-parity-closure.test.ts` extended in place (3 corrected/new assertions for Item 1's actual behavior, 1 new assertion for Item 3's heading fix -- 22 tests total in that file, up from 20) | None added/retargeted/removed; `no-new-react-sections-validator`, `validate-design-system`, `theme-governance-validator` all re-run clean | DONE — human/ARB seam unchanged from S3 (CAP-C08 updated further, not closed) | uncommitted working tree |
| PHASE-M5 | Closed check (c)'s remaining coverage gap in `payment-rail-allowlist-validator.mjs`: it scanned `apps`/`services/api-gateway`/`packages/checkout-core`/`packages/shared-types` but never `services/webhook-service`, `services/queue-workers`, or `services/mobile-bff`, even though check (a) already did (Phase M2 closed this class of gap for `packages/**` only). **W1** -- widened check (c) to the same roots as check (a); a raw scan (all exemptions disabled) surfaced exactly 9 offender lines across 4 files, reported before any application file was touched, matching the evidence table exactly: `gateway-billing-transitions.ts:48,90` (Billing domain), `gateway-order-transitions.ts:1` (`GatewayPaymentProvider`), `index.ts:111` (`PaymentProvider`), `index.ts:461` (`activateStoreSubscription`'s inline type). **W2** -- narrowed `GatewayPaymentProvider` and `PaymentProvider` to the single literal `"razorpay"` (not `"razorpay"/"cod"` -- webhooks are inherently provider-specific, there is no "COD webhook"), after tracing every usage transitively: both types are used exclusively by `markOrderPaid`/`markOrderFailed`/`markOrderRefunded`, called only from the surviving `/razorpay` handler (re-confirmed fresh via `grep -n '^app\.post('` -- exactly 3 routes, one payment-provider webhook) with hardcoded `"razorpay"` literals throughout, including `writeWebhookEvent`'s one real write into `payment_webhook_events.provider`. `activateStoreSubscription`'s `"razorpay" \| "stripe"` (`index.ts:461`) turned out to be Billing-domain, not Payments -- it calls `activateGatewayStoreSubscription`, imported from `gateway-billing-transitions.ts`, to activate a store's SUBSCRIPTION plan (LetBuyy invoicing merchants, not a customer checkout rail). A whole-file exclusion was wrong for this construct (`index.ts` also holds genuinely Payments-domain code), so added a new, distinctly-named `BILLING_DOMAIN_MARKER` (kept separate from `HISTORICAL_READ_PATH_MARKER`, which means something different) for construct-level billing exemptions inside a mixed-domain file. `emitPaymentDomainEvent` -- generic cross-domain infra shared by order events AND subscription events (its own `aggregateType: "order" \| "subscription"` already shows this) -- needed the same treatment: its `provider` param is deliberately `PaymentProvider \| "stripe"`, marked and explained. Added `gateway-billing-transitions.ts` to `BILLING_DOMAIN_EXCLUSIONS` (whole-file, matching its api-gateway equivalents). **W3** -- decided AGAINST adding a CHECK on `payment_webhook_events.provider`: its one writer is a hardcoded `"razorpay"` literal, not attacker/input-controlled, already protected by the same-file TS narrowing; `upsertSupabaseRows` throws on any non-OK response, so a constraint violation would surface as an unhandled exception in the `/razorpay` handler -- likely a 500 Razorpay retries indefinitely. The retry-storm downside outweighs the marginal defense-in-depth for a column no external input can reach today (unlike M4's cases, where multiple differently-typed call sites created real gaps a single TS type couldn't close). **W4** -- rewrote the pass message and header comments to name check (c)'s actual roots and both exemption mechanisms, replacing the prior uncalibrated blanket "across apps/services/packages" claim. **W5** -- negative control against the newly-covered `services/webhook-service/src` root: a scratch `.ts` file with a hand-written union observed FAILING and naming the file, then deleted and observed PASSING. **W6** -- webhook-service test suite run: 7/7 pass in `gateway-events.test.mjs` + `payment-webhook-retirement.test.mjs`; `gateway-billing-transitions.test.mjs` and `gateway-order-transitions.test.mjs` fail with the same pre-existing "does not provide an export" module-loading quirk documented in earlier phases -- confirmed NOT caused by this phase's edits by observing the identical failure in the untouched `gateway-billing-transitions.test.mjs`. | None | None | Negative control only (not a committed test file): a scratch `.ts` union under `services/webhook-service/src/__scratch__/`, proven to FAIL the validator, then deleted and the validator re-run to confirm PASS | `payment-rail-allowlist-validator` (widened, still passes; raw-scan re-verified, every offender accounted for); `canonical-ledger-only-validator`, `sequential-migration-validator` (re-run clean) | DONE | `2cd8da35` |
| PHASE-S4-EXPERIENCE-PRESETS | Materialised the 10 `theme-presets.ts` Experiences (Forge/Sense/Aura/Craft/Refresh/Ride/Dawn/Bloom/Nova/Summit) as real theme-level presets inside the Core Liquid bundle's `settings_data.json`, so the merchant-facing preset switcher is backed by the same bundle the storefront actually renders, not a parallel in-memory-only structure. **W1** -- added `buildLiquidThemeExperiencePresets()` to `theme-bundle.ts` (imports `listExperiences`/`buildExperienceSettingsOverlay` from `theme-presets.ts`; no circular-import risk, confirmed `theme-presets.ts` imports nothing back from `theme-bundle.ts`), wired as `settings_data.presets` sibling to the existing `current` block. Declared the 4 previously-undeclared overlay keys the schema was missing (`secondaryColor`, `backgroundColor`, `textColor`, `borderRadius`) across `settings_schema.colors`/new `appearance` group, converging field NAMES with migration 197 (`197_letbuyy_core_first_class_theme.sql`) without editing it. **W2** -- regenerated both example bundles (`node --import tsx/esm packages/theme-engine/examples/generate-examples.mjs`, never hand-edited); confirmed via `find files/sections files/blocks -type f` + `cmp` loop that zero section/block files changed byte-for-byte, only `config/settings_data.json` + `config/settings_schema.json` + the two manifest/bundle/index roll-ups legitimately differ. Verified `settings_data.json` now carries exactly the 10 named presets, each deep-equal to `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay(experience)`'s live output (spot-checked Forge token-for-token), no SEO/content key leakage into any preset. **W3** -- investigated whether the merchant editor's preset switcher (`ExperiencePicker.tsx` + `applyExperience()` in `.../online-store/themes/[themeId]/editor/page.tsx`) needed new code to pick up the bundle presets; found `applyExperience()` already calls `getExperience(experienceId)` + `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay(experience)` directly and shallow-merges into `themeSettings`, entirely independent of `settings_data.presets` -- the switcher already worked end-to-end before this phase. Wrote unit tests proving A-then-B preset switches land cleanly on B, content/SEO survive a preset switch, and no second preset system exists (grepped `ExperiencePicker.tsx` for `listExperiences`, editor page for `getExperience`/`buildExperienceSettingsOverlay`) instead of writing unneeded code -- "no code needed" was the correct outcome here, not a shortfall. **Decisions made and reasoning**: (1) colors stay `type: 'color'`, not 197's `'text'` -- the bundle's own pre-existing 3 color fields were already `'color'`, changing them would regress the merchant's native color-picker UX for zero benefit, and moving 197's typing is a migration-authority change out of this phase's scope; (2) `borderRadius` converges fully (name + `type: 'number'`) with 197 since no pre-existing bundle field conflicted; (3) `isDark`/`enableShadows`/`enableAnimations` (present in 197, absent from every Experience) were deliberately NOT declared -- no Experience token references them, declaring unused fields would be speculative scope creep; (4) `fontFamily` (present in the overlay, historically absent from the bundle both before and after this phase) was left undeclared as an intentional React-only alias -- `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay`'s own comment states it emits `fontFamily` for the "React JSON-contract path" and `bodyFontFamily` (which IS declared) for the "Liquid token map"; adding it would create a redundant second body-font control; (5) `urban-kicks-liquid` (reference-only theme) received the identical 10 presets -- same shared object literal, lower complexity than forking, and harmless since it is not the canary theme. | `packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-s4-experience-presets.test.ts` (new, 25 tests: W1 schema-declaration coverage, W2 preset-content/parity/no-leak/byte-identity coverage, W3 apply-preserves-content/switch-lands-clean/no-second-system coverage) | `packages/theme-engine/src/theme-bundle.ts` (import + `buildLiquidThemeExperiencePresets()` + 4 new schema fields + `appearance` settings group + `settings_data.presets` wiring), `packages/theme-engine/examples/{letbuyy-core-liquid,urban-kicks-liquid}/{bundle.json,manifest.json,files/config/settings_data.json,files/config/settings_schema.json}`, `packages/theme-engine/examples/index.json` (all regenerated, not hand-edited) | None -- no new migration; field-name convergence with 197 is additive-only inside the existing bundle schema | `theme-governance-validator`, `one-system-per-domain-validator`, `no-new-react-sections-validator`, `validate-design-system-validator`, `theme-editor-dead-shell-validator` (all re-run clean); full 111-validator sweep PASS=110 FAIL=1 (only the documented individual-run false-failure `stale-artifact-validator`; `branch-lag-guard-validator` did not fail this run) | DONE | UNCOMMITTED |
| PHASE-S4-FIX | Closed 3 defects the S4 self-audit found in S4's own uncommitted work: an undeclared key silently written into every preset, an inert second copy of the preset data the merchant editor never actually read, and no guard against the two drifting apart silently. **Defect 1 (undeclared `fontFamily`)** -- `buildLiquidThemeExperiencePresets()` copied `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay()`'s full 10-key output into every preset unfiltered, so all 10 presets carried `fontFamily` even though `settings_schema.json` never declared it (confirmed: S4's own mandatory test only passed because it excluded `fontFamily` from its own check via `REACT_ONLY_OVERLAY_KEYS`, rather than removing the key from the data -- migration 193's header names this exact failure class, "themeSettings.primaryColor is not defined by the theme settings schema"). Fixed at the EMISSION BOUNDARY only: hoisted the bundle's settings_schema object literal into a local `settingsSchema` const inside `createUrbanKicksLiquidThemeBundleFixture()` (previously inline in the returned literal, unreachable before construction) so `buildLiquidThemeExperiencePresets(settingsSchema)` can derive its allowed-key set from `collectDeclaredSettingKeys(settingsSchema)` -- a schema-derived filter, not a hardcoded denylist, so a future overlay key with no matching schema field is dropped automatically rather than silently written in undeclared. `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay` itself is untouched and proven so by a new direct test (still emits all 10 keys, including `fontFamily`, for every Experience) -- the shared function's contract (also used by the document-level Phase-O apply engine and the AI intent/personalization paths) never changed, only what theme-bundle.ts copies out of it. Deleted `REACT_ONLY_OVERLAY_KEYS` and its `continue` from the test file; the "every key declared" test now runs against the actual emitted presets with zero exclusions. **Defect 2 (single source)** -- traced the merchant editor's live apply path (`ExperiencePicker.tsx` + `applyExperience()`, `.../editor/page.tsx:803`) and confirmed it called `getExperience(experienceId)` + `buildExperienceSettingsOverlay(experience)` directly from the Experience registry, entirely bypassing `settings_data.presets` -- the bundle presets S4 built were real but inert; editing `theme-presets.ts` and forgetting to regenerate would never surface anywhere the merchant could see. Implemented decision (a) (values from the bundle, display metadata resolved by name), the prompt's recommended option: added `resolveExperiencePresetItems(bundlePresets)` to `theme-presets.ts`, which enumerates `settings_data.presets` (the bundle -- authoritative for which presets exist and what values they carry) and enriches each by NAME lookup against `EXPERIENCE_REGISTRY` for description/industry/swatches/heading-font/section-count; a bundle preset absent from the registry still resolves with a generic fallback (swatches derived from its own color-looking values, `id` a slug of its name); a registry Experience absent from the bundle does not resolve at all. `ExperiencePicker.tsx` no longer imports or calls `listExperiences()` -- it takes `presets: ExperiencePresetItem[]` as a prop and renders an explained empty state (not a blank panel) when the array is empty. The editor page's `applyExperience(presetId)` now looks the preset up in the resolved `presetItems` list (a `useMemo` over `presetsFromBaseConfig(baseConfig)`, mirroring `buildDraftConfig`'s own existing config-then-root precedence for reading `presets`) and merges `preset.values` -- read straight from the bundle, never recomputed from the registry -- into `themeSettings`; `experienceId` in `themeSettings` now stores the resolved preset's `id`, matched-registry id when available else the name slug. `getExperience`/`buildExperienceSettingsOverlay` imports were removed from `page.tsx` entirely -- zero remaining references confirmed by grep. Kept the content-safety contract unchanged (the merge can still only overwrite the same presentational keys it always could, since `preset.values` is post-Defect-1-filtered and still by construction excludes metaDescription/socialImageUrl/faviconUrl); did not touch `experience-apply.ts`'s STYLE_ONLY/COMPOSE_ADDITIVE mode contract or which mode the picker uses (it uses neither -- confirmed the picker was never wired to the Phase-O document-level engine, only to the bundle-level `theme-presets.ts` functions). No second preset system was created -- one JSON-record type (`ExperiencePresetItem`) added to the existing `theme-presets.ts` module, consumed by the existing picker/apply path; `one-system-per-domain-validator` re-run clean. **Defect 3 (drift guard)** -- replaced the plain `assert.deepEqual(committed.presets, live...)` check with a named "DRIFT GUARD" test asserting the live count is exactly 10, every live name matches a registry Experience name, AND the committed example JSON matches the live fixture, with a failure message modeled on the tenant-isolation-validator's own style: names the exact fix command (`node --import tsx/esm packages/theme-engine/examples/generate-examples.mjs`) instead of leaving a bare diff. Verified this guard actually catches drift, not just theoretically: temporarily edited a token in a scratch copy of the registry call path (not committed) and observed the guard fail with that exact message, then reverted. Test file grew from S4's 25 tests to 33 (net +8: removed 1 pre-fix test + its exclusion set, added 2 Defect-1 tests, replaced 1 W2 test with the drift guard, added 6 Defect-2 tests, rewrote the "no second system" test for the new wiring). Regenerated both example bundles via `generate-examples.mjs` (never hand-edited); re-verified `files/sections`/`files/blocks` byte-identical between bundles (zero `cmp` output) before and after. `letbuyy-core` re-confirmed still DARK (`liquid-s1-w3-letbuyy-core-attach.test.ts`, 4/4). | `packages/theme-engine/test/liquid-s4-experience-presets.test.ts` (rewritten in place: 33 tests, up from 25) | `packages/theme-engine/src/theme-bundle.ts` (`settingsSchema` hoisted to a local const; `buildLiquidThemeExperiencePresets` now schema-filtered), `packages/theme-engine/src/theme-presets.ts` (`resolveExperiencePresetItems` + `ExperiencePresetItem` added), `apps/merchant-control-plane/src/components/theme-editor/ExperiencePicker.tsx` (takes `presets` as a prop; empty state), `apps/merchant-control-plane/src/app/[store]/online-store/themes/[themeId]/editor/page.tsx` (`presetsFromBaseConfig`, `presetItems` memo, `applyExperience` rewritten to read bundle values), `packages/theme-engine/examples/{letbuyy-core-liquid,urban-kicks-liquid}/{bundle.json,manifest.json,files/config/settings_data.json,files/config/settings_schema.json}`, `examples/index.json` (all regenerated, not hand-edited) | None | Same file as W1 col (test file rewritten, not added separately) | `theme-governance-validator`, `one-system-per-domain-validator`, `no-new-react-sections-validator`, `validate-design-system` (ratchet improved further, not regressed: e.g. `tsxInlineStyles` 673→672), `theme-editor-dead-shell-validator` (all re-run clean); full 111-validator sweep PASS=110 FAIL=1 (only `stale-artifact-validator`, the documented individual-run false-failure; `branch-lag-guard-validator` did not fail) — tied S4's best-of-programme result | DONE | UNCOMMITTED |
